# Research Topic
Accelerating Graph Attention Network training

# Idea Info
{
    "Open Problems": "1. Training-time memory bottleneck: node-wise attention scores, soft-max buffers and gradients dominate GPU RAM, forcing small batch-sizes or sub-optimal neighbor sampling.\n2. Compute bottleneck: O(E·H) linear algebra is still dominated by dense GEMMs on full-precision activations; structured sparsity of Ampere/Turing GPUs is unused.\n3. Existing memory savers (GACT, ACTNN) are generic; they ignore graph topology and the fact that (a) most nodes have few neighbours, (b) attention distributions are extremely peaked for low-degree nodes.\n4. Mixed-precision / quant-aware methods (Degree-Quant, A2Q) work for inference only and quantise messages but not attention logits; they are not end-to-end differentiable during training.\n5. Sampling / sketching methods reduce compute but break the autograd graph or degrade convergence when combined with quantisation.\n\nWe lack a single, end-to-end differentiable pipeline that (i) compresses activations, (ii) exploits sparsity of attention maps, (iii) assigns precision adaptively per node/edge, and (iv) keeps the stochastic gradient unbiased.",
    "Methods": "Proposed system: GATSY (Graph Attention Training with Sparser Yet-precise gradients)\nA. Dual–domain Compression\n   A1  Edge–wise Top-k Masking: for every target node keep only the k=max(⌈α·deg⌉,1) largest softmax logits (α≈0.2). Mask is computed with a two-phase LSH scan similar to HyperAttention and cached; gradients are routed only through kept edges → O(k) memory.\n   A2  Quantised Residual Buffer: remaining logits/values are stored with adaptive per-edge bitwidth b_e∈{2,3,4,8}. b_e is selected by a lightweight learned policy π(deg,|v|,layer) trained with REINFORCE to minimise estimated variance subject to a global bit-budget (extension of A2Q/DQ ideas to activations).\nB. Structure-aware Activation Compression\n   B1  Integrate GACT controller but feed it the masked+quantised tensors. Sensitivity estimator is recalibrated on the masked graph; computational graph unchanged – unbiased gradient (proved by conditional expectation).\nC. Hardware-friendly Sparsity\n   C1  During backward we apply 2:4 semi-structured pruning to Value gradients using the same CUDA kernel as \"Accelerating Transformer Pre-training with 2:4 Sparsity\". Mask is recalculated online from magnitude so accuracy loss is <0.1%.\nD. Variance-controlled Neighbour Sampling\n   D1  For huge graphs we combine with GraphSAINT style node minibatches but analytic variance of masked+quantised estimator is used to adapt sample size so SGD step variance stays below threshold σ².\nE. Theory  – prove unbiasedness and give bound on additional variance ≤ ε when α≤0.3, b_e ≥2.\n",
    "Experimental Setup": "Datasets: OGBN-Arxiv, OGBN-Products, Reddit, PPI. Models: 2-layer and 4-layer GAT, GATv2. Baselines: Full-precision GAT, GACT (L2), Degree-Quant INT4, GraphSAINT, Spexphormer (sparse attention).\nMetrics: peak GPU memory, epoch wall-clock, convergence (test accuracy), FLOPs, additional variance.\nHardware: single A100 40 GB and RTX3090 24 GB.\nAblations: (i) no masking, (ii) fixed 4-bit, (iii) no 2:4 sparsity, (iv) no variance-controlled sampling.",
    "Experimental Code": "Key components (pseudo-python):\nclass EdgeMask(nn.Module):\n    def forward(q,k,deg):\n        scores=(q@k.T)/sqrt(dh)\n        topk_idx=lsh_topk(scores,k=max(1,int(alpha*deg)))\n        return topk_idx\n\nclass BitPolicy(nn.Module):\n    def forward(edge_feat):\n        logits=self.mlp(edge_feat.detach())\n        probs=Categorical(logits=logits)\n        bit=(probs.sample()+2)   # 2..8 bits\n        self.log_prob=probs.log_prob(bit-2)\n        return bit\n\n# integrate into torch.autograd graph\ndef quantised_message(v,bit):\n    return gact.quantize(v,bit)\n\n# REINFORCE loss added each adapt_interval steps\npolicy_loss = (variance_est.detach()*bit_policy.log_prob).mean()\n",
    "Expected Result": "1. Peak memory ↓ 6-8× vs FP32, 2-3× vs plain GACT.\n2. Throughput ↑ 2-3× vs GACT, ≥5× vs FP32 GAT on A100.\n3. Accuracy drop <0.3% on OGBN-Arxiv, none on Reddit; same final accuracy as FP32 when α≤0.25.\n4. Training converges 1.4× faster epochs-to-accuracy due to larger batch enabled by memory savings.\n5. Ablation shows masking contributes 50% memory save, adaptive bits 30%, 2:4 sparsity 20% compute speed.",
    "Expected Conclusion": "Edge-level adaptive precision coupled with coarse top-k masking provides the missing piece for truly scalable GAT training. GATSY demonstrates that memory-compute trade-offs can be orchestrated without harming convergence, achieving near-transformer-level efficiency for graph attentional models. The approach is orthogonal to future hardware advances and can be extended to heterophilic or temporal graphs."
}

# Novelty
GATSY is the first training-time scheme that (1) prunes attention maps on-the-fly with a learned, topology-aware top-k mask, (2) chooses an edge-wise bit-width by a REINFORCE policy while still keeping the whole autograd graph unbiased, and (3) plugs the resulting masked-and-quantised tensors into an existing generic compressor (GACT) and a 2:4 gradient‐sparsifier – none of the cited works do all three together. GACT compresses every activation uniformly; Degree-Quant/A2Q only quantise messages and only for inference; Spexphormer/HyperAttention sparsify attention but not gradients nor precision. The theoretical proof of unbiasedness under joint masking + quantisation and the variance-controlled GraphSAINT sampling are also new.

# Significance
If the reported numbers hold, GATSY reduces peak GPU memory 6-8× versus FP32 and 2-3× over the strongest training baseline (GACT), while giving 2-3× throughput on A100 with <0.3 % accuracy loss. This enables full-batch or large-mini-batch GAT/GATv2 training on single-GPU cards that previously required multi-GPU or out-of-core tricks, directly addressing the “Accelerating GAT training” theme. The method is orthogonal to hardware, backward-compatible with PyTorch, and its components (mask, bit-policy, 2:4 kernel) can be adopted independently. Hence practical impact for both academia and industry is high, though gains are limited to attention-based GNNs and rely on Ampere-style sparsity support.

{
    "Title": "GACT: Activation Compressed Training for Generic Network Architectures",
    "Main Contributions": "GACT is a generic Activation Compressed Training (ACT) framework designed to support a broad range of machine learning tasks and neural network (NN) architectures without requiring prior domain knowledge. The core problem addressed is the extensive memory requirement for training large NN models, which existing ACT methods failed to address universally. GACT's main contributions include a general convergence theory for ACT, proved by analyzing a linearized version of its approximate gradient, which ensures stability without assumptions on operator type or model architecture. It introduces an adaptive algorithm that estimates tensor sensitivity at runtime to automatically determine the optimal compression ratio for each tensor. Implemented as a PyTorch library, GACT significantly reduces activation memory by up to 8.1x and enables training with 4.2x to 24.7x larger batch sizes with negligible accuracy loss across diverse tasks like image classification, object detection, text, and graph node classification.",
    "Methodology": "GACT formulates ACT as a computational graph of operators, abstracting forward propagation into loss and context functions. It provides a general convergence theory by showing the stochastic gradient (SG) can be approximated by an unbiased, linearized version. The variance of this linearized gradient is characterized by both sampling and compression noise. The framework incorporates an adaptive algorithm to determine per-tensor compression rates. This algorithm estimates the sensitivity (cl) of each context tensor by periodically running a numerical algorithm (Algorithm 1) that computes empirical variance using two forward/backward passes with fixed and varied random seeds for compressors. The optimal compression scheme is then approximated by solving an integer programming problem, minimizing total variance under a given bit budget, using an O(Llog2L) greedy algorithm. GACT employs a per-group quantizer as its compressor. The system is implemented as a PyTorch library using low-level hooks to capture context tensors, supporting arbitrary and custom operators. It includes optimizations like parallel swap and prefetch for CPU offloading, and can be combined with gradient checkpointing and memory-efficient self-attention techniques.",
    "Experimental Setup": "GACT was evaluated across a wide range of machine learning tasks and models: Image Classification (VGG-11, ResNet-50, Swin-Tiny on ImageNet), Object Detection (RetinaNet, Faster R-CNN on COCO), NLP (Bert-large on GLUE benchmark including MNLI, QQP, SST-2, QNLI), and Graph Node Classification (GCN, GAT, GCNII on Flickr, Reddit, Yelp, ogbn-arxiv datasets). The performance was compared against full precision (FP32) training and other memory-saving baselines like ActNN, DTR, Mesa, ZeRO-Offload, and Gradient Checkpointing. Metrics included validation accuracy/F1-score, activation compression rate, training throughput, and maximum model scalability (depth, width, resolution). Experiments were conducted on an AWS g4dn.4xlarge instance equipped with a 16GB NVIDIA T4 GPU and 64GB CPU memory, using PyTorch 1.10. Stability was assessed by running multiple seeds (10 for graph tasks, 3 for NLP tasks) and reporting mean ±std of accuracy/F1.",
    "Limitations": "One limitation is the potential for training divergence when using very aggressive compression, such as 2-bit GACT on certain tasks (e.g., object detection), which can necessitate a reduction in the learning rate to ensure convergence. The paper highlights that GACT does not quantize all intermediate states, such as intermediate indices for max-pooling layers or running mean/variance used by batch normalization. This can limit the overall memory reduction in some network architectures (e.g., 78% of saved tensors in VGG11 for a specific batch size were unquantized max-pooling indices). Additionally, while deemed negligible in experiments, combining GACT with gradient checkpointing might theoretically introduce more training noise because recomputation starts from quantized segment inputs, making the recompute forward pass not exact.",
    "Future Research Directions": "Future research could explore strategies to improve convergence stability with extremely low-bit compression (e.g., 2-bit) without compromising training speed, possibly by investigating adaptive learning rate schemes or more robust gradient estimators under high compression error. Expanding GACT to quantize a broader range of intermediate states, such as max-pooling indices and BatchNorm running statistics, could further enhance memory reduction. Investigating the integration of alternative or more advanced compressor designs within the generic GACT framework could also be beneficial. Additionally, providing more rigorous theoretical guarantees for the convergence and noise properties of GACT when combined with other memory optimization techniques, particularly where recomputation relies on quantized values, could be a valuable direction.",
    "Experiment Code": "File Path: gact/gact/__init__.py\nContent:\nfrom . import ops\nfrom . import controller\nfrom .conf import config, set_optimization_level, set_adapt_interval\nFile Path: gact/gact/autoprec.py\nContent:\nimport os\nimport random\n\nimport torch\nimport torch.nn as nn\nimport numpy as np\nfrom gact.utils import exp_recorder, uniform_sample\nimport gact.cpp_extension.calc_precision as ext_calc_precision\n\n# Automatically compute the precision and update quantization bits for each tensor\nclass AutoPrecision:\n    def __init__(self, model, quantizer, bits, max_bits,\n                 work_dir, adapt_interval, log_interval,\n                 momentum=0.99, warmup_iter=100, debug=False):\n        self.model = model\n\n        self.quantizer = quantizer\n        self.debug = debug\n\n        self.dims = None\n        self.bits = {}  # bits for each tensor\n        self.C = {}  # Sensitivity for each tensor\n\n        self.avg_bits = bits\n        self.max_bits = max_bits\n\n        # For maintaining batch_grad and detecting overly large quantization variance\n        self.momentum = momentum\n        self.warmpup_iter = warmup_iter\n        self.beta1 = 1e-7\n        self.batch_grad = 0\n        self.grad_var = 0\n        self.adapt_interval = adapt_interval\n\n        self.iter = 0\n        self.log_iter = log_interval\n        self.work_dir = work_dir\n\n    def init_from_dims(self):\n        self.dims = self.quantizer.dims\n        total_ele = 0\n        for l in self.dims:\n            self.C[l] = 1.0\n            self.bits[l] = self.avg_bits\n            total_ele += self.dims[l]\n        self.total_bits = self.avg_bits * total_ele\n\n    def iterate_wrapper(self, backprop):\n        if self.dims is None:\n            self.init_from_dims()\n        self.iterate(backprop)\n\n    # This is the main logic to run auto precision\n    # backprop: instruct auto precision how to run forward and backward\n    # every adapt_interval, the function will recalculate the sensitivity\n    # for different activations and update quantization bits based on the sensitivity\n    def iterate(self, backprop):\n        def sample_grad():\n            grad = []\n            for param in self.model.parameters():\n                if param.grad is not None:\n                    grad.append(param.grad.ravel())\n            return torch.cat(grad, 0)\n\n        def setup_seed(seed):\n            random.seed(seed)\n            np.random.seed(seed)\n            torch.manual_seed(seed)\n            torch.cuda.manual_seed(seed)\n            torch.cuda.manual_seed_all(seed)\n\n        def get_bn(model):\n            bns = []\n            for _, child in model.named_children():\n                if isinstance(child, nn.BatchNorm1d) or \\\n                    isinstance(child, nn.BatchNorm2d) or \\\n                        isinstance(child, nn.BatchNorm3d):\n                    bns.append(child)\n                else:\n                    ret = get_bn(child)\n                    bns += ret\n            return bns\n\n        def get_grad():\n            bn_layers = get_bn(self.model)\n            # does not update running stats (running mean/var)\n            for bn in bn_layers:\n                bn.track_running_stats = False\n\n            org_state = torch.get_rng_state().clone()\n            setup_seed(self.iter)\n            backprop()\n            torch.set_rng_state(org_state)\n\n            for bn in bn_layers:\n                bn.track_running_stats = True\n            grad = sample_grad()\n            self.quantizer.iterate()\n\n            return grad\n\n        if self.iter % self.adapt_interval == 0:\n            # Do full adaptation\n            print('GACT: Initializing AutoPrec..., run extra %d iters' %\n                  (len(self.quantizer.bits)))\n            # use different random seeds to update snesitivity\n            for l in self.quantizer.bits:\n                b = self.quantizer.bits[l]\n                self.quantizer.bits[l] = 1\n                grad0 = get_grad()\n                self.quantizer.seeds[l] += 1\n                grad1 = get_grad()\n                self.quantizer.seeds[l] -= 1\n                self.quantizer.bits[l] = 8\n                grad2 = get_grad()\n                diff01 = ((grad0 - grad1) ** 2).sum()\n                diff02 = ((grad0 - grad2) ** 2).sum()\n                diff12 = ((grad1 - grad2) ** 2).sum()\n                sens = max(diff01, max(diff02, diff12)*2)\n                del grad0\n                del grad1\n                del grad2\n                if torch.isnan(sens) or torch.isinf(sens) or sens > 1e10:\n                    self.C[l] = 1e10\n                else:\n                    self.C[l] = sens.item()\n                self.quantizer.bits[l] = b\n            # refresh bits based on sensitivity\n            self.refresh_bits()\n\n        if self.debug and self.iter % self.adapt_interval == 0:\n            # Debug information\n            grad = get_grad()\n            for l in self.quantizer.bits:\n                self.quantizer.bits[l] = 32\n            # exact sensitivity calculated with 32 bits\n            det_grad = get_grad()\n            ap_var = ((grad - det_grad)**2).sum()\n            C_list = []\n            bits_list = []\n            for l in self.C:\n                C_list.append(self.C[l])\n                bits_list.append(self.bits[l])\n            predicted_var = (np.array(C_list) * 2 **\n                             (-2.0 * (np.array(bits_list) - 1))).sum()\n            print('Exact Variance %f, Predicted Variance %f' %\n                  (ap_var.item(), predicted_var.item()))\n\n            for b in [4]:\n                quant_var = 0\n                self.quantizer.bits = self.bits.copy()\n                for l in self.quantizer.bits:\n                    self.quantizer.bits[l] = b\n                grad = get_grad()\n                var = ((grad - det_grad)**2).sum()\n                quant_var += var\n                predicted_var = (np.array(list(self.C.values()))\n                                 * 2 ** (-2.0 * (b - 1))).sum()\n                print('use %d bit to compare, exact variance %f, predicted variance %f' % (\n                    b, quant_var.item(), predicted_var.item()))\n\n            self.quantizer.bits = self.bits.copy()\n\n        if self.log_iter > 0 and self.iter % self.log_iter == 0:\n            det_grad = get_grad()\n\n            # Maintain batch grad\n            momentum = self.momentum\n            self.beta1 = self.beta1 * momentum + 1 - momentum\n            self.batch_grad = self.batch_grad *\n                momentum + (1 - momentum) * det_grad\n            bgrad = self.batch_grad / self.beta1\n            gvar = ((bgrad - det_grad)**2).sum()\n            self.grad_var = self.grad_var * momentum + (1 - momentum) * gvar\n\n            # log sensitivity information\n            exp_recorder.record(\"iter\", self.iter)\n            exp_recorder.record(\"layer sensitivity\", self.C)\n            exp_recorder.record(\"bits\", self.bits)\n            exp_recorder.record(\"dims\", self.dims)\n            if not os.path.isdir(self.work_dir):\n                os.mkdir(self.work_dir)\n            exp_recorder.dump(self.work_dir + \"autoprec.log\")\n\n        self.iter += 1\n    \n    # refresh bits based on sensitivity\n    def refresh_bits(self):\n        dims_list = []\n        C_list = []\n        l_list = []\n        for l in self.dims:\n            l_list.append(l)\n            dims_list.append(self.dims[l])\n            C_list.append(self.C[l])\n\n        bits_tensor = torch.ones(\n            len(self.bits), dtype=torch.int32) * self.max_bits\n        bits_tensor = ext_calc_precision.calc_precision(bits_tensor,\n                                                        torch.tensor(\n                                                            C_list, dtype=torch.float),\n                                                        torch.tensor(\n                                                            dims_list, dtype=torch.int64),\n                                                        self.total_bits)\n\n        for i, l in enumerate(l_list):\n            self.bits[l] = bits_tensor[i].item()\n\n        self.quantizer.bits = self.bits.copy()\n\n        # Warning if the quantization variance is too large\n        if self.log_iter > 0 and self.iter > self.warmpup_iter:\n            overall_var = self.grad_var / self.beta1\n            C_list = []\n            bits_list = []\n            for l in self.C:\n                C_list.append(self.C[l])\n                bits_list.append(self.bits[l])\n            quantization_var = (np.array(C_list) * 2 **\n                                (-2.0 * np.array(bits_list))).sum()\n            if quantization_var > overall_var * 0.1:\n                print(\"========================================\")\n                print('GACT Warning: Quantization variance is too large. Consider increasing number of bits.',\n                      quantization_var, overall_var)\n                exp_recorder.record(\"iter\", self.iter)\n                exp_recorder.record(\"layer sensitivity\", self.C)\n                exp_recorder.record(\"bits\", self.bits)\n                exp_recorder.record(\"dims\", self.dims)\n                exp_recorder.record(\"warning\", True)\n                exp_recorder.record(\"quantization var\",\n                                    quantization_var.tolist())\n                exp_recorder.record(\"overall var\", overall_var.tolist())\n                exp_recorder.dump(self.work_dir + \"autoprec.log\")\n                print(\"========================================\")\nFile Path: gact/gact/conf.py\nContent:\ndef = min(k_chunk_size, num_kv)\n        num_key_chunk = math.ceil(num_kv / key_chunk_size)\n        query = query / math.sqrt(k_features)\n\n        def summarize_chunk(query, key, value):\n            attn_weights = torch.einsum('bhqd,bhkd->bhqk', query, key)\n            max_score = torch.max(attn_weights, axis=-1, keepdims=True).values\n            max_score = max_score.detach()\n            exp_weights = torch.exp(attn_weights - max_score)\n            exp_values = torch.einsum('bhvf,bhqv->bhqf', value, exp_weights)\n            exp_values = dropout(exp_values)\n            return (exp_values, exp_weights.sum(axis=-1), max_score.squeeze())\n\n        chunk_values = None\n        chunk_weights = None\n        global_max = None\n\n        def batch_dot(m1, m2):\n            feature_size = m1.shape[-1]\n            v = m1.reshape(-1, feature_size) * m2.reshape(-1, 1)\n            return v.reshape(m1.shape)\n\n        for i in range(num_key_chunk):\n            key_chunk = tensors\"\"\"\n    if not isinstance(tensors, (list, tuple)):\n        tensors = [tensors]\n\n    ret = 0\n    for x in tensors:\n        if type(x)== int:\n            ret += 4\n        elif x.dtype in [torch.long]:\n            ret += np.prod(x.size()) * 8\n        elif x.dtype in [torch.float32, torch.int]:\n            ret += np.prod(x.size()) * 4\n        elif x.dtype in [torch.bfloat16,     "Experiment Result": "GACT implements an adaptive compression framework with the following key experimental settings and capabilities:\n\n1.  **Quantization Scheme**: GACT uses a per-group quantizer with a configurable `group_size` (default 256). Activation tensors are quantized to a specified `bit` precision (e.g., 2, 4, 8 bits) using `op_quantize` and dequantized using `op_dequantize`. Dropout masks are specially handled with 1-bit quantization via `op_quantize_mask` and `op_dequantize_mask`.\n\n2.  **Adaptive Precision (AutoPrec)**: The framework includes an `AutoPrecision` module (`gact/gact/autoprec.py`) to dynamically determine per-tensor compression rates. This is enabled by setting `config.auto_prec = True`. Key parameters include:\n    *   `config.bit`: The average or initial number of bits for activations (e.g., 2, 3, 4 bits, depending on the optimization level).\n    *   `config.max_bit`: The maximum number of bits a tensor can be quantized to (default 32).\n    *   `config.adapt_interval`: The frequency (in iterations) at which the adaptive algorithm re-calculates tensor sensitivities and updates bit allocations (default 1000, can be set to 20 in GNN benchmarks).\n    *   **Sensitivity Estimation**: The `AutoPrecision.iterate` method estimates the sensitivity (empirical variance `C`) of each context tensor. This is done by performing two forward/backward passes: one with a fixed random seed and one with a varied random seed for the compressors. It also probes sensitivity by temporarily setting bits to 1 and 8 to calculate `diff01`, `diff02`, `diff12` and determine `sens`.\n    *   **Bit Allocation**: `AutoPrecision.refresh_bits` uses `ext_calc_precision.calc_precision` (a C++/CUDA extension) to solve an integer programming-like problem. This allocates bits to minimize total quantization variance under a given bit budget, based on the estimated sensitivities.\n    *   `config.log_interval` and `config.debug`: Control logging and debugging of sensitivity estimations.\n\n3.  **Memory Management Optimizations (Swap and Prefetch)**:\n    *   `config.swap`: When enabled, quantized activation tensors are offloaded to CPU memory (`pin_memory=True`) to reduce GPU memory footprint.\n    *   `config.prefetch`: When enabled alongside `swap`, activation tensors for the next backward pass are prefetched from CPU to GPU using asynchronous CUDA streams (`swap_in_stream`, `swap_out_stream`) to overlap data transfer with computation.\n\n4.  **Integration and Hooks**: GACT is implemented as a PyTorch library and integrates by using low-level hooks (`torch.autograd.graph.saved_tensors_hooks`) within the `Controller` class (`gact/gact/controller.py`) to intercept and compress/decompress context tensors saved for backward passes.\n\n5.  **Optimization Levels (`conf.set_optimization_level`)**: Predefined configurations for GACT are available:\n    *   `L0`: Disables activation compression.\n    *   `L1`, `L1.1`, `L1.2`: Fixed-bit quantization (4-bit, 8-bit, 2-bit respectively), without auto-precision.\n    *   `L2`, `L2.1`, `L2.2`: Auto-precision with target average bits (4-bit, 3-bit, 2-bit respectively).\n    *   `L3`: Auto-precision (4-bit) with swap and prefetch enabled.\n    *   `swap`: Enables vanilla swap without activation compression.\n    *   `L4bit-swap`: Fixed 4-bit quantization with swap and prefetch enabled.\n\n6.  **Combination with Other Techniques**:\n    *   **Gradient Checkpointing**: GACT can be combined with gradient checkpointing. This is explicitly enabled via `--use-checkpoint` in Swin-Transformer and `--ckpt` in Text Classification benchmarks, and BERT models can use `model.gradient_checkpointing_enable()`.\n    *   **Memory-Efficient Self-Attention**: The `ops.self_atten` function provides an implementation of efficient self-attention, which can be used in conjunction with GACT for further memory savings.\n\n7.  **Experimental Data Collection**: The `gact.utils.exp_recorder` is used to log detailed experimental results including memory usage (total, activation, peak), IPS (images per second/iterations per second), batch size, network architecture, and GACT algorithm level (`exp_recorder.dump('speed_results.json')`, `exp_recorder.dump('mem_results.json')`). Benchmarks also include arguments to specifically measure `--get-speed`, `--get-mem`, and `--get-macs`."
}{
    "Title": "GACT: Activation Compressed Training for Generic Network Architectures",
    "Main Contributions": "GACT introduces a generic Activation Compressed Training (ACT) framework designed for a broad range of neural network architectures, addressing the substantial memory demands of training large models. Its core contributions include a novel general convergence theory for ACT, proven without requiring prior knowledge of operator types or model architecture. Furthermore, GACT features an adaptive algorithm that dynamically estimates the sensitivity of each tensor and determines its optimal compression ratio at runtime. The framework is implemented as an efficient PyTorch library with an easy-to-use API, facilitating seamless integration with other memory-saving techniques. This approach significantly reduces activation memory by up to 8.1x, enabling training with 4.2x to 24.7x larger batch sizes with negligible accuracy loss across diverse machine learning tasks.",
    "Methodology": "GACT formalizes ACT by representing neural networks as computational graphs and abstracting forward propagation into loss and context functions. Its methodology is based on: 1) **General Convergence Theory**: Establishing convergence by linearizing the approximate AC gradient g(Q(h); θ) using a first-order Taylor expansion ˆg(Q(h); h,θ) = g(h; θ) + J(h,θ)∆h. This linearized gradient is shown to be unbiased, allowing standard SGD analytical tools to apply by analyzing its variance. 2) **Adaptive Compression Rate Algorithm**: A numerical algorithm is developed to automatically estimate the sensitivity (cl(h,θ)) of each context tensor. This involves computing the empirical variance of the gradient by performing two forward and backward passes, fixing random seeds for all compressors except the target tensor. 3) **Optimal Bit Allocation**: The algorithm approximately solves an integer programming problem to minimize gradient variance under a total bit budget, using a greedy O(L log2 L) approach. This relies on assumptions about the compressor, such as element-wise uncorrelated results and a structured compression error (e.g., from stochastic rounding quantizers). 4) **PyTorch Implementation**: GACT is built as a lightweight PyTorch library, utilizing low-level hooks to capture and compress context tensors from any operator, including custom ones. It intelligently filters to quantize only activations, avoiding parameters and training states, and uses tensor footprints to prevent redundant quantization. 5) **Memory Optimization Integrations**: GACT combines with parallel swapping (offloading compressed tensors to CPU with prefetching using separate CUDA streams), gradient checkpointing (quantizing segment inputs and recomputed activations), and memory-efficient self-attention techniques for quadratic space complexity reduction in transformers.",
    "Experimental Setup": "GACT was rigorously evaluated on various machine learning tasks using an AWS g4dn.4xlarge instance equipped with a 16GB NVIDIA T4 GPU and 64GB CPU memory, running PyTorch 1.10. The evaluation encompassed: 1) **Image Classification**: VGG-11, ResNet-50, and Swin-Tiny models on ImageNet. 2) **Object Detection**: RetinaNet and Faster R-CNN on the Coco dataset. 3) **Text Classification**: Bert-large on GLUE benchmark datasets (MNLI, SST-2, MRPC, QNLI). 4) **Graph Node Classification**: GCN, GAT, and GCNII models on Flickr, Reddit, Yelp, and ogbn-arxiv datasets. Comparisons were made against full precision (FP32) training and several baselines: ActNN (quantization), DTR (rematerialization), Mesa (transformer-specific memory saving), ZeRO-Offload (optimizer state offload), and simple swapping and gradient checkpointing strategies. GACT was tested with different adaptive bit settings (4, 3, 2 bits) and optimization levels (L1: adaptive quantization; L2: L1 + swapping; CB1: L1 + gradient checkpointing; CB2: CB1 + efficient self-attention). Performance was measured by validation accuracy/F1-score, activation compression rate, training throughput (TFLOPS, sequence/s), maximum model scalability (depth, width, resolution), and detailed memory usage (peak and total). Robustness was assessed through multiple runs (up to 10 for graph tasks, 3 for NLP) with reported mean ± standard deviation.",
    "Limitations": "GACT faces several limitations: 1) **Divergence at High Compression**: Aggressive compression rates, such as 2-bit quantization, can lead to training divergence for certain tasks (e.g., object detection) if the learning rate is not appropriately reduced to counteract the increased compression error. The study notes that maintaining a fixed learning rate as in full precision training can cause divergence when compression error is substantial. 2) **Variable Memory Reduction**: The actual memory reduction achieved by GACT varies across network architectures because it deliberately does not quantize all intermediate states. For instance, in VGG-11, intermediate indices for max-pooling layers, which are not quantized, can still consume a significant portion of memory. 3) **Potential Training Noise with Checkpointing**: While generally negligible in experiments, combining GACT with gradient checkpointing might introduce additional training noise due to the recomputation phase starting from quantized segment inputs, making the forward pass of recompute not entirely exact. 4) **Task-Specific Robustness**: The robustness of extreme compression (e.g., 2-bit) shows task- and model-specific variations, performing better for certain graph models compared to others.",
    "Future Research Directions": "Not mentioned",
    "Experiment Code": "File Path: gact/gact/__init__.py\nContent:\nfrom . import ops\nfrom . import controller\nfrom .conf import config, set_optimization_level, set_adapt_interval\n\nFile Path: gact/gact/autoprec.py\nContent:\nimport os\nimport random\n\nimport torch\nimport torch.nn as nn\nimport numpy as np\nfrom gact.utils import exp_recorder, uniform_sample\nimport gact.cpp_extension.calc_precision as ext_calc_precision\n\n# Automatically compute the precision and update quantization bits for each tensor\nclass AutoPrecision:\n    def __init__(self, model, quantizer, bits, max_bits,\n                 work_dir, adapt_interval, log_interval,\n                 momentum=0.99, warmup_iter=100, debug=False):\n        self.model = model\n\n        self.quantizer = quantizer\n        self.debug = debug\n\n        self.dims = None\n        self.bits = {}  # This is the main logic to run auto precision\n    # backprop: instruct auto precision how to run forward and backward\n    # every adapt_interval, the function will recalculate the sensitivity\n    # for different activations and update quantization bits based on the sensitivity\n    def iterate(self, backprop):\n        def sample_grad():\n            grad = []\n            for param in self.model.parameters():\n                if param.grad is not None:\n                    grad.append(param.grad.ravel())\n            return torch.cat(grad, 0)\n\n        def setup_seed(seed):\n            random.seed(seed)\n            np.random.seed(seed)\n            torch.manual_seed(seed)\n            torch.cuda.manual_seed(seed)\n            torch.cuda.manual_seed_all(seed)\n\n        def get_bn(model):\n            bns = []\n            for _, child in model.named_children():\n                if isinstance(child, nn.BatchNorm1d) or \\\n                    isinstance(child, nn.BatchNorm2d) or \\\n                        isinstance(child, nn.BatchNorm3d):\n                    bns.append(child)\n                else:\n                    ret = get_bn(child)\n                    bns += ret\n            return bns\n\n        def get_grad():\n            bn_layers = get_bn(self.model)\n            # does not update running stats (running mean/var)\n            for bn in bn_layers:\n                bn.track_running_stats = False\n\n            org_state = torch.get_rng_state().clone()\n            setup_seed(self.iter)\n            backprop()\n            torch.set_rng_state(org_state)\n\n            for bn in bn_layers:\n                bn.track_running_stats = True\n            grad = sample_grad()\n            self.quantizer.iterate()\n\n            return grad\n\n        if self.iter % self.adapt_interval == 0:\n            # Do full adaptation\n            print('GACT: Initializing AutoPrec..., run extra %d iters' %\n                  (len(self.quantizer.bits)))\n            # use different random seeds to update snesitivity\n            for l in self.quantizer.bits:\n                b = self.quantizer.bits[l]\n                self.quantizer.bits[l] = 1\n                grad0 = get_grad()\n                self.quantizer.seeds[l] += 1\n                grad1 = get_grad()\n                self.quantizer.seeds[l] -= 1\n                self.quantizer.bits[l] = 8\n                grad2 = get_grad()\n                diff01 = ((grad0 - grad1) ** 2).sum()\n                diff02 = ((grad0 - grad2) ** 2).sum()\n                diff12 = ((grad1 - grad2) ** 2).sum()\n                sens = max(diff01, max(diff02, diff12)*2)\n                del grad0\n                del grad1\n                del grad2\n                if torch.isnan(sens) or torch.isinf(sens) or sens > 1e10:\n                    self.C[l] = 1e10\n                else:\n                    self.C[l] = sens.item()\n                self.quantizer.bits[l] = b\n            # refresh bits based on sensitivity\n            self.refresh_bits()\n\n        if self.debug and self.iter % self.adapt_interval == 0:\n            # Debug information\n            grad = get_grad()\n            for l in self.quantizer.bits:\n                self.quantizer.bits[l] = 32\n            # exact sensitivity calculated with 32 bits\n            det_grad = get_grad()\n            ap_var = ((grad - det_grad)**2).sum()\n            C_list = []\n            bits_list = []\n            for l in self.C:\n                C_list.append(self.C[l])\n                bits_list.append(self.bits[l])\n            predicted_var = (np.array(C_list) * 2 **\n                             (-2.0 * (np.array(bits_list) - 1))).sum()\n            print('Exact Variance %f, Predicted Variance %f' %\n                  (ap_var.item(), predicted_var.item()))\n\n            for b in [4]:\n                quant_var = 0\n                self.quantizer.bits = self.bits.copy()\n                for l in self.quantizer.bits:\n                    self.quantizer.bits[l] = b\n                grad = get_grad()\n                var = ((grad - det_grad)**2).sum()\n                quant_var += var\n                predicted_var = (np.array(list(self.C.values()))\n                                 * 2 ** (-2.0 * (b - 1))).sum()\n                print('use %d bit to compare, exact variance %f, predicted variance %f' % (\n                    b, quant_var.item(), predicted_var.item()))\n\n            self.quantizer.bits = self.bits.copy()\n\n        if self.log_iter > 0 and self.iter % self.log_iter == 0:\n            det_grad = get_grad()\n\n            # Maintain batch grad\n            momentum = self.momentum\n            self.beta1 = self.beta1 * momentum + 1 - momentum\n            self.batch_grad = self.batch_grad *\n                momentum + (1 - momentum) * det_grad\n            bgrad = self.batch_grad / self.beta1\n            gvar = ((bgrad - det_grad)**2).sum()\n            self.grad_var = self.grad_var * momentum + (1 - momentum) * gvar\n\n            # log sensitivity information\n            exp_recorder.record(\"iter\", self.iter)\n            exp_recorder.record(\"layer sensitivity\", self.C)\n            exp_recorder.record(\"bits\", self.bits)\n            exp_recorder.record(\"dims\", self.dims)\n            if not os.path.isdir(self.work_dir):\n                os.mkdir(self.work_dir)\n            exp_recorder.dump(self.work_dir + \"autoprec.log\")\n\n        self.iter += 1\n    \n    # refresh bits based on sensitivity\n    def refresh_bits(self):\n        dims_list = []\n        C_list = []\n        l_list = []\n        for l in self.dims:\n            l_list.append(l)\n            dims_list.append(self.dims[l])\n            C_list.append(self.C[l])\n\n        bits_tensor = torch.ones(\n            len(self.bits), dtype=torch.int32) * self.max_bits\n        bits_tensor = ext_calc_precision.calc_precision(bits_tensor,\n                                                        torch.tensor(\n                                                            C_list, dtype=torch.float),\n                                                        torch.tensor(\n                                                            dims_list, dtype=torch.int64),\n                                                        self.total_bits)\n\n        for i, l in enumerate(l_list):\n            self.bits[l] = bits_tensor[i].item()\n\n        self.quantizer.bits = self.bits.copy()\n\n        # Warning if the quantization variance is too large\n        if self.log_iter > 0 and self.iter > self.warmpup_iter:\n            overall_var = self.grad_var / self.beta1\n            C_list = []\n            bits_list = []\n            for l in self.C:\n                C_list.append(self.C[l])\n                bits_list.append(self.bits[l])\n            quantization_var = (np.array(C_list) * 2 **\n                                (-2.0 * np.array(bits_list))).sum()\n            if quantization_var > overall_var * 0.1:\n                print(\"========================================\")\n                print('GACT Warning: Quantization variance is too large. Consider increasing number of bits.',\n                      quantization_var, overall_var)\n                exp_recorder.record(\"iter\", self.iter)\n                exp_recorder.record(\"layer sensitivity\", self.C)\n                exp_recorder.record(\"bits\", self.bits)\n                exp_recorder.record(\"dims\", self.dims)\n                exp_recorder.record(\"warning\", True)\n                exp_recorder.record(\"quantization var\",\n                                    quantization_var.tolist())\n                exp_recorder.record(\"overall var\", overall_var.tolist())\n                exp_recorder.dump(self.work_dir + \"autoprec.log\")\n                print(\"========================================\")\n\nFile Path: gact/gact/conf.py\nContent:\ndef set_optimization_level(level):\n    if level == 'L0':      # Do nothing\n        config.compress_activation = False\n    elif level == 'L1':    # fixed 4-bit\n        config.auto_prec = False\n        config.bit = 4\n    elif level == 'L1.1':    # fixed 8-bit\n        config.auto_prec = False\n        config.bit = 8\n    elif level == 'L1.2':    # fixed 2-bit\n        config.auto_prec = False\n = True\n        config.bit = 3\n    elif level == 'L2.2':  # auto precision 2-bit\n        config.auto_prec = True\n        config.bit = 2\n    elif level == 'L3':  # auto precision 4-bit + swap\n = min(k_chunk_size, num_kv)\n        num_key_chunk = math.ceil(num_kv / key_chunk_size)\n        query = query / math.sqrt(k_features)\n\n        def summarize_chunk(query, key, value):\n            attn_weights = torch.einsum('bhqd,bhkd->bhqk', query, key)\n            max_score = torch.max(attn_weights, axis=-1, keepdims=True).values\n            max_score = max_score.detach()\n            exp_weights = torch.exp(attn_weights - max_score)\n            exp_values = torch.einsum('bhvf,bhqv->bhqf', value, exp_weights)\n            exp_values = dropout(exp_values)\n            return (exp_values, exp_weights.sum(axis=-1), max_score.squeeze())\n\n        chunk_values = None\n        chunk_weights = None\n        global_max = None\n\n        def batch_dot(m1, m2):\n \"\"\"\n    default_bit: the number of bits used to quantize\n    swap: if turned on, swap activation memory to CPU\n    prefetch: if turned on, activation of the previous layer will be prefetched. the parameter is meaningful only when swap is True\n    \"\"\"\n\n    def __init__(self, default_bit, swap, prefetch):\n        self.unrelated_tensors = set()  # record the tensors that should not be including the extra inter for auto precision\n        # iteration for seed, share the same seed_iter for the same auto precision adaptive step\n        self.seed_iter = 0\n\n    def differently because it can be quantized with 1 bit with a specialized kernel\n    def check_quantize(self, input_tensor):\n        # does not quantize parameters\n        if input_tensor.data_ptr() in self.unrelated_tensors:\n            return False, False\n        # special check for saved mask\n        if input_tensor.numel() > 0 and input_tensor.dtype == torch.uint8:\n            if (input_tensor.max() == 1) and (input_tensor.min() == 0):\n                return True, True\n            return False, False\n        # only quantize float16 and float32\n        if input_tensor.dtype not in [torch.float32, torch.float16]:\n            return False, False\n        # only quantize activation that requires gradient\n        # for example: BN statistics (running mean/var) should not be quantized\n        if input_tensor.requires_grad is False:\n            return False, False\n        # only quantize 2/3/4D tensors for now\n        if ((len(input_tensor.shape) != 2)\n            and (len(input_tensor.shape) != 3)\n            and (len(input_tensor.shape) != 4)\n            ):\n            return False, False\n        return True, False\n\n    def __del__(self):\n        del tensors\"\"\"\n    if not isinstance(tensors, (list, tuple)):\n        tensors = [tensors]\n\n    ret = 0\n    for x in tensors:\n        if type(x)== int:\n            ret += 4\n        elif x.dtype in [torch.long]:\n            ret += np.prod(x.size()) * 8\n        elif x.dtype in [torch.float32, torch.int]:\n            ret += np.prod(x.size()) * 4\n        elif x.dtype in [torch.bfloat16, torch.float16, torch.int16]:\n            ret += np.prod(x.size()) * 2\n        elif x.dtype in [torch.int8, torch.uint8]:\n            ret += np.prod(x.size()) * 1\n        else:\n            print(\"[Error] unsupport datatype \", x.dtype)\n            exit(0)\n\n    return ret\n\ndef empty_cache(ratio):\n    if ratio is None:\n        return\n    allocated = def __init__(self):\n        self.val_dict = OrderedDict()\n\n    def record(self, key, value, float_round=6):\n        if isinstance(value, (np.int32, np.int64)):\n            value = int(value)\n        if isinstance(value, (float, np.float32, np.float64)):\n            value = round(value, float_round)\n\n        self.val_dict[key] = value\n\n    def dump(self, filename):\n        with len(y_true)\n\n\nbatch_total_time = 1\ntrain_ips_list = []\n# install hook\nwith torch.autograd.graph.saved_tensors_hooks(pack_hook, unpack_hook):\n    batch_time = AverageMeter('Time', ':6.3f')\n losses.update(loss.detach().item())\n\n        if get_mem and i > 0:\n            print(\"===============Before Backward=======================\")\n            before_backward =            num_nodes, train_ips, 1000.0 / train_ips)\n                print(res, flush=True)\n                exp_recorder.record(\"network\", 'gcn')\n                if args.gact:\n 0:\n            print(\"===============After Backward=======================\")\n            after_backward = get_memory_usage(True)  # model size\n            # init : weight + optimizer state + data size\n            # before backward : weight + optimizer state + data size + activation + loss + output\n            # after backward : init + grad\n            # grad = weight\n            # total - act = weight + optimizer state + data size + loss + output + grad\n            total_mem.update(before_backward + (after_backward - init_mem))\n            peak_mem.update(\n                torch.cuda.max_memory_allocated())\n            activation_mem.update(\n                before_backward - after_backward)\n            break\n\n        del loss\n        del output\n\n        def optimizer.zero_grad()\n            loss.backward()\n            return loss, output\n\n        if quantize:\n            controller.iterate(get_grad)\n\n    model = best_model\n    model.eval()\n Setup logging, we only want one process per machine to log things on the screen.\n    # accelerator.is_local_main_process is only True for one process per machine.\n    logger.setLevel(logging.INFO if accelerator.is_local_main_process else logging.ERROR)\n    if accelerator.is_local_main_process:\n        datasets.utils.logging.set_verbosity_warning()\n        transformers.utils.logging.set_verbosity_info()\n    else:\n        datasets.utils.logging.set_verbosity_error()\n        transformers.utils.logging.set_verbosity_error()\n\n    # For CSV/JSON files, this script will use as labels the column called 'label' and as pair of sentences the\n    # sentences in columns called 'sentence1' and 'sentence2' if such column exists or the first two columns not named\n    # label if at least two columns are provided.\n\n    # If the CSVs/JSONs contain only one non-label column, the script does single sentence classification on this\n    # single column. You can easily tweak this behavior (see below)\n\n    # In distributed training, the load_dataset function guarantee that only one local process can concurrently\n    # download the dataset.\n    if args.task_name is not None:\n        # See more about loading any type of standard or custom dataset at\n    # https://huggingface.co/docs/datasets/loading_datasets.html.\n\n    # Labels\n    if args.task_name is not None:\n        is_regression = args.task_name == \"stsb\"\n        if not is_regression:\n            label_list = raw_datasets[\"train\"].features[\"label\"].names\n            num_labels = len(label_list)\n        else:\n            num_labels = 1\n    else:\n        # Trying to have good defaults here, don't hesitate to tweak to your needs.\n        is_regression = raw_datasets[\"train\"].features[\"label\"].dtype in [\"float32\", \"float64\"]\n        if is_regression:\n            num_labels = 1\n        else:\n            # A useful fast method:s\n            # https://huggingface.co/docs/datasets/package_reference/main_classes.html#datasets.Dataset.unique\n            label_list = raw_datasets[\"train\"].unique(\"label\")\n            label_list.sort()  # Let's sort it for determinism\n            num_labels = len(label_list)\n\n    tokenizer = AutoTokenizer.from_pretrained(args.model_name_or_path, use_fast=not args.use_slow_tokenizer)\n    # Load pretrained model and tokenizer\n    #\n    # update optimization level, this is only for logging output\n    if args.ckpt and args.gact:\n        args.opt_level += '_ckpt'\n    elif args.ckpt:\n        args.opt_level = 'ckpt'\n            \n    # Preprocessing the datasets\n    if args.task_name is not None:\n        sentence1_key, sentence2_key = task_to_keys[args.task_name]\n    else:\n        # Again, we try to have some nice defaults but don't hesitate to tweak to your use case.\n        non_label_column_names = [name for name in raw_datasets[\"train\"].column_names if name != \"label\"]\n        if \"sentence1\" in non_label_column_names and \"sentence2\" in non_label_column_names:\n            sentence1_key, sentence2_key = \"sentence1\", \"sentence2\"\n        else:\n            if len(non_label_column_names) >= 2:\n                sentence1_key, sentence2_key = non_label_column_names[:2]\n            else:\n                sentence1_key, sentence2_key = non_label_column_names[0], None\n\n    # Some models have set the order of the labels to use, so let's make sure we do use it.\n    label_to_id = None\n    if (\n        model.config.label2id != PretrainedConfig(num_labels=num_labels).label2id\n        and args.task_name is not None\n        and not is_regression\n    ):\n        # Some have all caps in their config, some don't.\n        label_name_to_id = {k.lower(): v for k, v in model.config.label2id.items()}\n        if list(sorted(label_name_to_id.keys())) == list(sorted(label_list)):\n            logger.info(\n                f\"The configuration of the model provided the following label correspondence: {label_name_to_id}. \"\n                \"Using it!\"\n            )\n            label_to_id = {i: label_name_to_id[label_list[i]] for i in range(num_labels)}\n        else:\n            logger.warning(\n                \"Your model seems to have been trained with labels, but they don't match the dataset: \",\n                f\"model labels: {list(sorted(label_name_to_id.keys()))}, dataset labels: {list(sorted(label_list))}..\"\n                \"\\nIgnoring the model labels as a result.\",\n            )\n    elif args.task_name is None:\n        label_to_id = {v: i for i, v in enumerate(label_list)}\n\n    if label_to_id is not None:\n        model.config.label2id = label_to_id\n        model.config.id2label = {id: label for label, id in config.label2id.items()}\n    elif args.task_name is not None and not is_regression:\n        model.config.label2id = {l: i for i, l in enumerate(label_list)}\n        model.config.id2label = {id: label for label, id in config.label2id.items()}\n\n    padding = \"max_length\" if args.pad_to_max_length else False\n\n    def preprocess_function(examples):\n        # Tokenize the texts\n        texts = (\n            (examples[sentence1_key],) if sentence2_key is None else (examples[sentence1_key], examples[sentence2_key])\n        )\n        result = tokenizer(*texts, padding=padding, max_length=args.max_length, truncation=True)\n\n        if \"label\" in examples:\n            if label_to_id is not None:\n                # Map labels to IDs (not necessary for GLUE tasks)\n                result[\"labels\"] = [label_to_id[l] for l in examples[\"label\"]]\n            else:\n                # In all cases, rename the column to labels because the model will expect that.\n                result[\"labels\"] = examples[\"label\"]\n        return result\n\n    with accelerator.main_process_first():\n        processed_datasets = raw_datasets.map(\n            preprocess_function,\n            batched=True,\n            remove_columns=raw_datasets[\"train\"].column_names,\n            desc=\"Running tokenizer on dataset\",\n        )\n\n    train_dataset = processed_datasets[\"train\"]\n    eval_dataset = processed_datasets[\"validation_matched\" if args.task_name == \"mnli\" else \"validation\"]\n\n    train_max_length = 0\n    dev_max_length = 0\n    for item in train_dataset:\n        if len(item['input_ids']) > train_max_length:\n            train_max_length = len(item['input_ids'])\n    for item in eval_dataset:\n        if len(item['input_ids']) > dev_max_length:\n            dev_max_length = len(item['input_ids'])\n    logger.info('Train max length: %d' % train_max_length)\n    logger.info('Dev max length: %d' % dev_max_length)\n\n    # Log a few random samples from the training set:\n    for index in random.sample(range(len(train_dataset)), 3):\n        logger.info(f\"Sample {index} of the training set: {train_dataset[index]}.\")\n\n    # DataLoaders creation:\n    if args.pad_to_max_length:\n        # If padding was already done ot max length, we use the default data collator that will just convert everything\n        # to tensors.\n        data_collator = default_data_collator\n    else:\n        # Otherwise, `DataCollatorWithPadding` will apply dynamic padding for us (by padding to the maximum length of\n        # the samples passed). When using mixed precision, we add `pad_to_multiple_of=8` to pad all tensors to multiple\n        # of 8s, which will enable the use of Tensor Cores on NVIDIA hardware with compute capability >= 7.5 (Volta).\n        data_collator = DataCollatorWithPadding(tokenizer, pad_to_multiple_of=(8 if accelerator.use_fp16 else None))\n\n    train_dataloader = DataLoader(\n        train_dataset, shuffle=True, collate_fn=data_collator, batch_size=args.per_device_train_batch_size\n    )\n    eval_dataloader = DataLoader(eval_dataset, collate_fn=data_collator, batch_size=args.per_device_eval_batch_size)\n\n    # Optimizer\n    # Split weights in two groups, one with weight decay and the other not.\n    no_decay = [\"bias\", \"LayerNorm.weight\"]\n    optimizer_grouped_parameters = [\n        {\n            \"params\": [p for n, p in model.named_parameters() if not any(nd in n for nd in no_decay)],\n            \"weight_decay\": args.weight_decay,\n        },\n        {\n            \"params\": [p for n, p in model.named_parameters() if any(nd in n for nd in no_decay)],\n            \"weight_decay\": 0.0,\n        },\n    ]\n    optimizer = AdamW(optimizer_grouped_parameters, lr=args.learning_rate, weight_decay=args.weight_decay)\n\n    # Prepare everything with our `accelerator`.\n    model, optimizer, train_dataloader, eval_dataloader = accelerator.prepare(\n        model, optimizer, train_dataloader, eval_dataloader\n    )\n\n    # Note -> the training dataloader needs to be prepared before we grab his length below (cause its length will be\n    # shorter in multiprocess)\n\n    # Scheduler and math around the number of training steps.\n    num_update_steps_per_epoch = math.ceil(len(train_dataloader) / args.gradient_accumulation_steps)\n    if args.max_train_steps is None:\n        args.max_train_steps = args.num_train_epochs * num_update_steps_per_epoch\n    else:\n        args.num_train_epochs = math.ceil(args.max_train_steps / num_update_steps_per_epoch)\n\n    lr_scheduler = get_scheduler(\n        name=args.lr_scheduler_type,\n        optimizer=optimizer,\n        num_warmup_steps=args.num_warmup_steps,\n        num_training_steps=args.max_train_steps,\n    )\n\n    # Get the metric function\n    if args.task_name is not None:\n        metric = load_metric(\"glue\", args.task_name)\n    else:\n        metric = load_metric(\"accuracy\")\n\n    # Train!\n    total_batch_size = args.per_device_train_batch_size * accelerator.num_processes * args.gradient_accumulation_steps\n\n    logger.info(\"***** Running training *****\")\n    logger.info(f\"  Num examples = {len(train_dataset)}\")\n    logger.info(f\"  Num Epochs = {args.num_train_epochs}\")\n    logger.info(f\"  Instantaneous batch size per device = {args.per_device_train_batch_size}\")\n    logger.info(f\"  Total train batch size (w. parallel, distributed & accumulation) = {total_batch_size}\")\n    logger.info(f\"  Gradient Accumulation steps = {args.gradient_accumulation_steps}\")\n    logger.info(f\"  Total optimization steps = {args.max_train_steps}\")\n    # Only show the progress bar once on each machine.\n    progress_bar = tqdm(range(args.max_train_steps), disable=not accelerator.is_local_main_process)\n    completed_steps = 0\n    total_mem = AverageMeter('Total Memory', ':.4e')\n    peak_mem = AverageMeter('Peak Memory', ':.4e')\n    activation_mem = AverageMeter('Activation Memory', ':.4e')\n\n    def pack_hook(tensor): # quantize hook\n        if args.gact:\n            return controller.quantize(tensor)\n        return tensor\n\n    def unpack_hook(tensor): # dequantize hook\n        if args.gact:\n            return controller.dequantize(tensor)\n        return tensor\n\n\n    iter = 0\n    best_metric = 0\n    batch_total_time = 0\n    with torch.autograd.graph.saved_tensors_hooks(pack_hook, unpack_hook):\n        for epoch in range(args.num_train_epochs):\n            model.train()\n            for step, batch in enumerate(train_dataloader):\n                iter += 1\n        \n                if args.get_mem and iter > 1:\n                    torch.cuda.synchronize()\n                    # accelerator.print(\"===============After Data Loading=======================\")\n                    init_mem = get_memory_usage(False)  # model size + data size\n                    torch.cuda.reset_peak_memory_stats()\n                \n                if args.get_speed and iter > 1:\n                    start = torch.cuda.Event(enable_timing=True)\n                    end = torch.cuda.Event(enable_timing=True)\n        \n                    torch.cuda.synchronize()\n                    start.record()\n                \n                for k, v in batch.items():\n                    batch[k] = v.to(args.device)\n                \n                if args.get_macs:\n                    from thop import profile\n                    inputs_for_flops = (\n                        batch.get(\"input_ids\", None),\n                        batch.get(\"attention_mask\", None),\n                        batch.get(\"token_type_ids\", None),\n                        batch.get(\"position_ids\", None),\n                        batch.get(\"head_mask\", None),\n                        batch.get(\"input_embeds\", None),\n                        batch.get(\"labels\", None),\n                    )\n                    macs, params = profile(model, inputs=inputs_for_flops,)\n\n                    print(f\"Macs: {macs}\\t Params: {params}\")\n                    out_file = \"get_macs.json\"\n                    with open(out_file, 'w') as fout:\n                        fout.write(json.dumps([macs, params]))\n                    print(f\"save results to {out_file}\")\n                    exit()\n                    \n                outputs = model(**batch)\n                loss = outputs.loss\n                loss = loss / args.gradient_accumulation_steps\n                if args.get_mem and iter > 1:\n                    # accelerator.print(\"===============Before Backward=======================\")\n                    torch.cuda.synchronize()\n                    before_backward = get_memory_usage(True)\n                optimizer.zero_grad()\n                loss.backward()\n                torch.utils.checkpoint.first_iter = False\n                if args.get_mem and iter > 1:\n                    # accelerator.print(\"===============After Backward=======================\")\n                    torch.cuda.synchronize()\n                    for t in batch:\n                        del t\n                    after_backward = get_memory_usage(False)  # model size\n                    # init : weight + optimizer state + data size + grad (iter > 1)\n                    # before backward : weight + optimizer state + data size + activation + loss + output + grad (iter > 1)\n                    # after backward : init\n                    # grad = weight\n                    # total - act = weight + optimizer state + data size + loss + output + grad\n                    total_mem.update(before_backward)\n                    activation_mem.update(before_backward - after_backward)\n                    peak_mem.update(\n                        torch.cuda.max_memory_allocated())\n                    del loss\n                    del outputs\n                    \n                    accelerator.print(\"peak %d MB\" % (peak_mem.get_value() / 1024 / 1024))\n                    accelerator.print(\"total %d MB\" % (total_mem.get_value() / 1024 / 1024))\n                    accelerator.print(\"activation %d MB\" % (activation_mem.get_value() / 1024 / 1024))\n                    exit(0)\n                    \n                if args.get_speed and iter > 1:\n                    end.record()\n                    torch.cuda.synchronize()\n                    cur_batch_time = start.elapsed_time(end) / 1000.0 # event in ms\n                    batch_total_time += cur_batch_time\n                    \n                    if iter == 6:\n                        bs = args.per_device_train_batch_size\n                        train_ips = 5 * bs / batch_total_time\n                        res = \"BatchSize: %d\\tIPS: %.2f\\t,Cost: %.2f ms\" % (\n                            bs, train_ips, 1000.0 / train_ips)\n                        print(res, flush=True)\n                        exp_recorder.record(\"network\", args.model_name_or_path)\n                        exp_recorder.record(\"algorithm\", args.opt_level)\n                        exp_recorder.record(\"batch_size\", bs)\n                        exp_recorder.record(\"ips\", train_ips, 2)\n                        exp_recorder.record(\"bacth_time\", cur_batch_time)\n                        exp_recorder.record(\"layer_num\", config.num_hidden_layers)\n                        exp_recorder.record(\"hidden_size\", config.hidden_size)\n                        exp_recorder.record(\"tstamp\", time.time(), 2)\n                        exp_recorder.dump('speed_results.json')\n                        exit(0)\n\n                # if step % args.gradient_accumulation_steps == 0 or step == len(train_dataloader) - 1:\n                torch.nn.utils.clip_grad.clip_grad_norm_(model.parameters(), args.max_gradient_norm)\n                optimizer.step()\n                lr_scheduler.step()\n                # optimizer.zero_grad()\n                progress_bar.update(1)\n                completed_steps += 1\n\n                if completed_steps >= args.max_train_steps:\n                    break\n\n                if args.gact:\n                    def backprop():\n                        small_batch = {}\n                        for k, v in batch.items():\n                            small_batch[k] = v[:8]\n                        outputs = model(**small_batch)\n                        loss = outputs.loss\n                        optimizer.zero_grad()\n                        loss.backward()\n                        del loss\n                        del outputs\n                        del small_batch\n                    controller.iterate(backprop)\n\nFile Path: benchmark/vision/train.py\nContent:\n\"\"\"\nModified from https://github.com/utsaslab/MONeT/blob/master/examples/imagenet.py\n\"\"\"\n\nimport argparse\nimport json\nimport os\nimport random\nimport shutil\nimport time\nimport warnings\n\nimport torch\nimport torch.nn as nn\nimport torch.nn.parallel\nimport torch.backends.cudnn as cudnn\nimport torch.optim\nimport torch.utils.data\nimport torchvision.transforms as transforms\nimport torchvision.datasets as datasets\nimport torchvision.models as models\nimport numpy as np\n\nfrom scaled_resnet import scaled_resnet, scaled_wide_resnet\nimport gact\nfrom gact import config\nfrom gact.utils import get_memory_usage, compute_tensor_bytes, exp_recorder\n\nMB = 1024**2\nGB = 1024**3\nMEM_LIMIT = 15 * GB\n\nmodel_names = sorted(name for name in models.__dict__\n                     if name.islower() and not name.startswith(\"__\")\n                     and callable(models.__dict__[name]))\n\nparser = argparse.ArgumentParser(description='PyTorch ImageNet Training')\nparser.add_argument('--data', metavar='DIR',\n                    help='path to dataset')\nparser.add_argument('-a', '--arch', metavar='ARCH', default='resnet18',\n                    # ')\nparser.add_argument('--gpu', default=None, type=int,\n                    help='GPU id to use.')\nparser.add_argument('--ablation', action=\"store_true\",\n                    help=\"Do ablation?\")\nparser.add_argument('--benchmark', type=str, default='exact')\nparser.add_argument('--limit', type=float, default=1, help=\"memory percentage\")\nparser.add_argument('--alg', type=str, default=\"L0\",\n                    help=\"gact optimization level, default does not quantize\")\nparser.add_argument('--input-size', type=int)\nparser.add_argument('--get_macs', action='store_true')\nparser.add_argument('--get_mem', action='store_true')\nparser.add_argument('--get_speed', action='store_true')\n\nbest_acc1 = 0\n\n\ndef main():\n    args = parser.parse_args()\n    \n    # Simply call main_worker function\n    main_worker(args)\n\n\ndef main_worker(args):\n    global best_acc1\n    # create model\n    if args.pretrained:\n        print(\"=> using pre-trained model '{}'\".format(args.arch))\n        model = models.__dict__[args.arch](pretrained=True)\n    else:\n        print(\"=> creating model '{}'\".format(args.arch))\n        if args.arch.startswith('scaled_wide_resnet'):\n            model = scaled_wide_resnet(args.arch)\n        elif args.arch.startswith('scaled_resnet'):\n            model = scaled_resnet(args.arch)\n        else:\n            if args.arch in ['inception_v3']:\n                kwargs = {\"aux_logits\": False}\n            else:\n                kwargs = {}\n            model = models.__dict__[args.arch](**kwargs)\n\n    model.cuda()\n\n    if args.get_mem:\n        print(\"========== Model Only ==========\")\n        usage = get_memory_usage(True)\n        exp_recorder.record(\"network\", args.arch)\n        exp_recorder.record(\"algorithm\", args.alg)\n        exp_recorder.record(\"model_only\", usage / GB, 2)\n\n    #
